LOCAL FIXTURE REVIEW

Pokémon Genesis prompts

The complete system instruction, the current world rules, and eight reproducible moments from the actual Pokémon controller.

Open complete system promptOpen complete review data

These are prompt examples from controlled fixtures, not generated clips. No paid model, video or image requests were made. Video descriptions are compiled from accepted controller outcomes. The model response for image-cache planning is mocked.

Five seconds is the playback target measured from a trainer's selection. An earlier urgent action, waiting for the other trainer, generation or network delays can make it late. The queue follows confirmed playback.

Reactor can replay an idle clip that has already appeared on screen when its scene still matches. Native H3 keeps the last frame visible until the new chunk is ready. It switches at a confirmed chunk boundary. Immediate hard cancellation and idle replay are not supported there.

The configured primary orchestrator is zai-org/GLM-5.3-Flash-ultra through the existing Lithos account. Unavailable providers fall back to the existing Gemini director. Current-state image caches can supply a later attack's start frame once ready and its exact before-action fingerprint matches.

1

Send out Charizard

Urgent. A ready controller frame starts with the red Pokémon position empty. The ball opens during this chunk. Battle begins is absent until both send-out clips finish.

urgentnew-frame5.17 second shotPlayback target +5s

Compiled video prompt

For the target video, at 0.00 seconds into the target video, <Picture 1> (from [Shot 1]) is fully referenced.

integrated_multimodal_description: [Shot 1] Overview: Hyperrealistic live-action creature cinematography, natural materials, luminous elemental effects and grounded weight. The red trainer sends out Charizard.

Near team: LOWER-LEFT quadrant, centered x25% y70%, rear three-quarter facing upper-right: empty Pokémon space at the opening. The red-jacket trainer stands directly behind it holding one closed red-and-white Poké Ball containing Charizard. Recognizable orange scaled dragon, cream belly, two horns, two arms, powerful hind legs, teal leathery wings and flame-tipped tail. Photographic reptile skin and translucent wing veins. The creature remains inside the closed ball at the opening.

Far team: UPPER-RIGHT quadrant, centered x76% y32%, front three-quarter facing lower-left: empty Pokémon space. The blue-jacket trainer waits behind it holding a closed Poké Ball. This battle position remains empty throughout the shot.

Camera and environment: Fixed 35mm camera pitched downward 65 degrees. Ground fills the frame; horizon and sky stay above the crop. Red is near-left foreground; blue is far-right background, several body lengths farther from the lens. Keep open diagonal ground between them. Rugged woodland floor, moss, sunlit mist and a clear diagonal attack lane.

Action: Begin this accepted action once within the first second. All spoken audio is in English. During the opening command, within the first second, the red-jacket trainer on the LEFT (S1), with a youthful bright mid-pitched voice, begins saying exactly once: <d>[English] Charizard, I choose you!</d> Only this trainer lip-syncs, then closes their mouth. The line finishes naturally. Other characters remain vocally silent. No extra words, narration or subtitles. No other vocal audio. Within the first second, the red-jacket trainer throws one red-and-white Poké Ball toward their own empty battle position. The ball opens with a brief white flash. Exactly one Charizard emerges and lands by approximately three seconds, then plants its feet, settles into a confident ready stance and holds through the end. The opposite team stays unchanged. No attack, damage or evolution. One continuous static shot at natural speed. At most two Pokémon, one per occupied side. No duplicate bodies. Physical scenery and characters only. No UI, HUD, health bars, numbers, labels, menus, captions, logos or watermarks.

overall_soundscape: Soft wind and leaf rustle. One ball throw swish, opening click and soft release shimmer follow the visible send-out.

non_diegetic_music: N/A
